I don’t really joke around with the regulars like I used to. After shifts I used to text my brother, just stupid stuff, but now I mostly just… leave messages sitting there. I say what I need to say and that’s about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… nothing I’ve really kept track of. Sleep’s been kind of just… whatever it is, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t have a plan to do anything to myself. But sometimes, when I’m lying there before an early shift, I think… maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like I wouldn’t have to keep messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Most days it just feels heavy, empty. Like I’m carrying plates and putting on the smile because that’s the job, not because I actually feel much of anything good coming.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this down nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days. It’s been like that for a while, I guess. Just this heavy, empty feeling, like I’m doing the work and smiling because I have to, not because I actually feel okay.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after the mortgage situation became a problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been going on since after I took on the condo mortgage. I’ve had low stretches before, I guess, but this heavy, empty feeling most days—just going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to—that’s more what it’s been like lately.\n\nDoctor: If the mortgage problem were resolved, do you think you’d still feel this down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. Like I’m just carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel good.\n\nDoctor: Before this started after the mortgage, was there ever another time in your life when you felt this same kind of depression most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really… not like this.
